## CQC apartment: rack guns at distance detail (headset check needed)
- Measured: the five rack guns are 308k triangles (160 meshes), more than the rest of the library view. aptRackLod (from aptRackCull, every frame): a gun or magazine resting on the rack (p.aptOnRack, set by arPut, cleared when a hand takes it) uses enemyLodGeo's simplified copies (cached by shape): full within 2 m (so always full within reach of a hand), 30 % to 5 m, 10 % beyond; back to full the frame it is taken. Copies are made in idle time (aptLodQueue, one geometry per 60 ms; full detail until they exist). enemyLodGeo now also simplifies mid-size parts (indexed from 400 triangles, non-indexed welded from 600 vertices; was 1000 / 3000), so the men's guns get lighter too.
- Headless: on the rack from 6 m 308k -> 64k triangles; within 2 m unchanged. Frustum culling (per chunk / per object), back-face cone culling, small-object culling (CONFIG.detail, pixel size) and the PVS were already in.

## Quest 3S performance guide (docs/QUEST3S_PERFORMANCE.md)
- What the game already does in VR (dynamic resolution, adaptive foveation, 72/80/90 Hz ladder, chunks, PVS, LODs, baked light, shader warm-up, texture streaming), supported knobs, a repeatable on-device test protocol (OVR Metrics Tool + `cqcHitches('vr')` over chrome://inspect, URL flags for A/B), experimental ADB properties (debug.oculus.cpuLevel / gpuLevel / foveation.level / refreshRate: whether the browser honours them is unverified; debug.* resets on reboot) and Quest Games Optimizer (native-game tool, little effect on WebXR). developers.meta.com could not be read from the build machine (DNS blocked), so official details are marked unverified.
- Checked: three.js has multiview only in its WebGPU renderer (r180's three.webgpu.js has OVR_multiview2, three.module.js / WebGLRenderer none). Multiview for these games = WebGPU/TSL port or a WebGLRenderer patch with gl_ViewID_OVR in every custom shader: the largest remaining VR CPU win, not started.

## CQC: multiview stereo in VR (vendored three.js patched; ?mv=off; headset check needed)
- One draw call renders both eyes. three.js's WebGLRenderer has no multiview (checked to r187; only WebGPURenderer), so `cqc/vendor/three/build/three.module.js` (r170) is patched (search "CQC patch"):
  - WebXRManager: `renderer.xr.multiview = true` before the session -> the projection layer is made with textureType 'texture-array' when OCULUS_multiview / OVR_multiview2 exist (falls back to the old layer if refused); its own framebuffer with framebufferTextureMultisampleMultiviewOVR (4x MSAA, OCULUS) or framebufferTextureMultiviewOVR, re-attached when the swapchain texture changes; own depth array if the layer has none. `renderer.xr.isMultiview` says it is in use.
  - Shading in one common view space (the XR ArrayCamera's: lights, vViewPosition, normals, viewMatrix, modelViewMatrix unchanged); `projectionMatrix` = P_eye x V_eye x V_common^-1 and `cameraPosition` per eye (`#define` to uniform arrays indexed by gl_ViewID_OVR, passed to the fragment shader as a flat varying via a wrapped main); lights_fragment_begin's view direction starts at each eye (mvEyeVS). Every custom shader works unchanged. Program cache flag `multiview` (from the current render target), program switch when it changes.
  - render(): an ArrayCamera into a multiview target draws the list once (no transmission pass in multiview).
  - Also fixed: readRenderTargetPixelsAsync left a PIXEL_PACK_BUFFER bound while waiting (the PVS bake keeps 3 in flight), which broke every other readPixels meanwhile.
- world/src/mirrors.js: in a multiview pass both eyes' reflections are drawn side by side and each eye reads its half (textureMatrices[VIEW_ID]). world/src/clusters.js: back-face cone culling keeps a cluster either eye can see. aptPvsCull accepts the ArrayCamera.
- CQC: MV (OPTIONS > GRAPHICS "Multiview stereo (VR)", ?mv=off, ?mv=on); renderer.render wrapped for the first 40 multiview frames of a session: a GL error raised by the frame's draw -> cqc.mv.fail, vrNote, off from the next session (turning the option on clears it).
- Test: cqc/tools/mvtest.html + mvtest.js (mvCompare: multiview into a 2-layer target vs each eye drawn alone, headless Chromium + SwiftShader exposes OVR_multiview2). Test scene (lights, shadows, clearcoat, instanced, batched, sprite, custom shader, planar mirror): max 1/255 except the mirror (reflection drawn at a different width). Real game, all programs linked, no GL errors: safe room 0 differing pixels; apartment library / great hall / bath 1 pixel of 204,800; Outpost 17 yard (3 views) 0. Not run on a Quest.

## CQC: 3D sound and bounce light in rooms (?portal=off, ?bounce=off; headset check needed)
- 3D sound: HRTF panning (above / below / behind) now in every mode but Max FPS on a computer (Sharp and the headset used equal-power panning; HRTF runs on the audio thread); voice pool 12 (was 8, voices were stolen mid-sound in a fight).
- Sound through doorways (SPORT, soundPortal from soundAt): a source behind a wall that one doorway connects to you (both legs clear at 1.7 m in the opening, within 25 m) is heard from that doorway at the path's length (delay and air loss too): clear through an open door (lowpass 7 kHz), muffled through a shut one (occludedHz x1.3). Otherwise straight through the wall as before. OPTIONS AUDIO "Sound through doorways". Checked headless: from the safe room, sources in the next room in view of the door get the door's position (path 8.65 m vs 8.63 straight), ones behind its partitions keep the old path.
- Bounce light (BNC, BOUNCE section before tfillTick): per CQC room (not stairwells, yard, apartment) a radiosity solve on the CPU in ~2 ms slices: the room box (floor, walls with the lower band, ceiling; albedo = the room's own material colour x map mean) cut into ~0.5 m patches, lit by every live ceiling fitting as a 30 cd cosine downlight (CONFIG.light.spot), two bounces, gathered into an L1 irradiance grid (up to 24 x 3 x 24 probes, 0.75 m) in one shared half-float Data3DTexture (its clone() returns itself so every program samples the same one). Patched into three's lights_fragment_maps for every lit material: inside the current room's box, ambient / env irradiance x 0.5 + the computed bounce x 3 (uBvK). Shot-out fittings re-bake the room; lights out (night) = off. OPTIONS GRAPHICS "Bounce light in rooms".
- Checked headless: all programs link, the safe room bakes (11 x 3 x 8 probes), before / after renders: the ceiling is lit by the floor (was black), upper corners darker, overall level +8 %. Not checked on Quest (3 texture fetches per lit pixel in rooms).

## CQC: props no longer fly off a touch (PUSH, ?push=off; headset check needed)
- Cause: your body capsule, the VR hand spheres and a held gun's probe are kinematic bodies (infinite mass) in Rapier, so a loose prop they touched took at least their speed and often more (solver overshoot): a brushing hand sent a 7 kg chair across the room.
- pushBefore / pushAfter round world.step in stepPhysics: props within 2.6 m of your head have their speed noted before the step; after it, a prop actually in contact (contactPair, numContacts > 0) with your body (75 kg), a hand (2.5 kg) or a held gun (4 kg) keeps at most its own speed + what a momentum exchange at restitution 0.25 gives it (source speed x M x 1.25 / (M + m)) + gravity + 5 cm/s; spin capped the same way. Source speeds from the kinematic targets' per-frame motion (PUSH.pv, hb.dv, probe dv); teleports count 0. Walking into a chair pushes it at your pace; a hand at 3 m/s moves a 7 kg chair ~1 m/s and still bats a 0.4 kg can at ~3 m/s.
- Checked: module parse, one headless apartment load (no errors). Not checked in a headset (feel).

## Apartment: everything opens (kitchen, nightstands, vanities, shelving cabinets; ?aptopen=off; headset check needed)
- Approved by the user ("make the stuff all openable"). world/src/furniture.js openables (opnPart: hinge / drawer / flap pivots, part built in its own frame): kitchen run doors and drawers (cutlery inserts, plates, pots, bottles, jars), sink bay with trap, upper bays (plates, glasses, bowls), oven doors (cavity, racks, lamp), French-door fridge (liner, glass shelves, crisper bins, door bins, freezer drawer), pantry doors (tins, jars, boxes, bottles); now also nightstands (hollow walnut case, two real drawers: books + watch box / linen + notebook), bath vanities (hollow carcass, a deep drawer with towels and a bottle, a shallow tray under each bowl with bottles and a dish) and the shelving base cabinets (hollow bays with a shelf and books, pairs of hinged doors with finger grooves). 77 parts in CQC. furnish returns `openables`.
- CQC (APARTMENT OPENABLES section at the end of cqc/index.html, AOPEN): aptOpenInit from aptBuilder. Computer: E / click with the dot on a part within 2.2 m (aptOpenDesk in termDeskPress; a prop nearer on the line wins, a wall between blocks it) eases it open / shut. Headset: squeeze within 13 cm of its handle (aptOpenGrip in squeezeStart after door levers): a drawer follows the hand along its run, a door turns about its hinge to the hand's angle, an oven door drops about its bottom edge; let go = stays; pulled 30 cm off the handle = let go; tick at either stop. Synth sounds (catch, runner, soft-close bump; fridge seal). No colliders on the moving parts (the carcass keeps its own). PVS extras (kind 'opn'): shut parts culled like the loose things, an open or held one always drawn (bake key changes: one re-bake).
- Checked headless (CQC ?dev&start=apt&lite=on): 77 parts (35 drawers, 35 cabinet doors, 2 oven, 2 fridge, 1 freezer, 2 pantry), desktop E opened a nightstand drawer 0.30 m, a simulated VR hand pulled cab / fridge / oven / drawer / pantry to 60 % along their paths, all 77 opened with no errors; renders of the nightstand, vanity, library and media cabinets opened. Not checked in a headset (grip reach, feel).

## Headset KTX2 textures (encoded, off until tested)
- world/city/quest/*.ktx2 (90 city faces, 154 MB) and world/textures/scans/quest/*.ktx2 (53 scans, 23 MB): UASTC via ktx2-encoder, y-flipped at encode. view.js / materials.js load them with three's KTX2Loader (vendored transcoder) when the switches are on: manifest questKtx2 and SCANS_KTX2, both false until a headset-path test (?q=quest&ktx2=force) passes.

## CQC: multiview off by default
- User report in the headset: fast black flashing and an inverted view. Multiview stereo (the only change to how each eye is drawn) is now opt-in: ?mv=on or OPTIONS > GRAPHICS "Multiview stereo (VR, experimental)". Default = the old two-pass stereo.
- Fix attempt (not verified on a Quest): the multiview projection layer is now made with no depth (depthFormat 0) and the pass draws into its own depth array. Before, the layer's depth texture was attached to the multisampled multiview framebuffer, and the compositor, which reprojects frames with the layer depth, could get it unresolved: a likely cause of the flashing and the view swimming the wrong way. Also: OVR_multiview2 is always enabled (the shaders need it), each array layer takes the view the headset maps to it (subImage.imageIndex), an incomplete framebuffer ends the session and switches multiview off, and a GL error in the first 40 frames ends the session at once (was: off from the next session). Still opt-in (?mv=on / OPTIONS) until checked in the headset.

## CQC: speed pass (lag / low fps report)
- Main cause found (CPU profile, apartment start): the apartment's visibility (PVS) bake ran while you played. Its key changed with the openables, so every player re-baked it: 624 cells, each 12 renders of the whole apartment, one every 40 ms on a computer and 220 ms in the headset, for minutes. Now the bakes ship with the game (cqc/data/pvs/<key>.json, computer and headset / Lite variants, made headless by loading the page with ?pvsbake and saving localStorage world.pvs); world/src/pvs.js fetches the matching file first (opts.url) and only bakes when none matches: never while you are in the headset, one cell per 300 ms otherwise. A geometry change to the apartment changes the key: re-bake both files (/tmp-style script: load cqc/index.html?dev&start=apt[&lite=on]&pvsbake, wait for aptPVS.ready, copy localStorage 'world.pvs' into data/pvs/<file>).
- Checked headless: a fresh profile loads the shipped file (cached, 0 cells baked); computer and Lite / headset give the same key, so one file. 69 inside meshes (of 225 in the 77 parts) hidden while shut, no part loses its front.
- Shut drawers, cabinet / fridge / pantry doors and the oven no longer draw what is behind their front (drawer boxes, plates, bottles, door bins): it.inner, shown from the first mm of opening.
- Bounce light in rooms is off in the headset and on Lite graphics (?bounce=on forces it on Lite).

## CQC: raid lag pass 1
- CPU profile of a Siege raid (headless, render stubbed): each spawned man's gun was built from its specs (rounded boxes, merges, UVs: ~1 s of work per wave on a computer, more on Quest). enemyGunBuild: one template per gun type, cloned (geometry / materials shared like a fresh build, parts remapped; falls back to a fresh build on any mismatch; ?egunclone=off = old).
- The hitch monitor read performance.memory every frame (~0.5 ms): now only on a hitch frame and every 20th frame. XR_LOW's user-agent regex is computed once.
- Raid pass 2 (same stubbed profile, Siege, 3-7 men; before -> after, ms over ~8 s): spawn work 299 -> 149, enemyPose 273 -> 135, hitch monitor 140 -> 28, no page errors. enemySkinSync reads the driver's world matrices directly (mwPos / mwQuat) and refreshes skin bones only up to the holder (mwChain) instead of getWorldPosition / getWorldQuaternion climbing to the scene root per bone. A man you can see 10 m or more away is posed at 36 Hz. A settled body woken by a touch goes back to sleep within 1.5 s (it used to stay awake for good: stillT stuck at -999). Physics step itself (~2 ms on a computer, 600 colliders) unchanged.

## CQC: Xbox controller (Edge on Xbox or any pad; ?pad=off)
- XBOX / GAMEPAD section before setExposure: padPoll (from tick, before updateDesktopMove) reads the first standard-mapping pad. Edge on Xbox: gamepadInputEmulation = 'gamepad' in play, 'mouse' on the title and while the watch menu is open (padEmu every 0.4 s), so the cursor clicks DOM buttons there.
- Map: LS move (click = run until the stick rests), RS look (squared response, slower aimed / scoped), RT fire or use (as the left mouse button), LT aim, A interact (E), X reload (hold: rack), B crouch (hold: hop), Y hip / chest gun swap (hold: night vision), RB frag / LB flashbang (hold + release), D-pad up light (hold: IR) / down laser / left selector / right injector, RS click kick, View shove (hold: help), Menu watch menu (Menu or B closes it). Buttons send the keyboard's own key events. Help panel has the table.
- Xbox user agent turns Lite graphics on by default. Checked headless with a mocked pad (look turns, no errors). Not tried on an Xbox.

## CQC: two-player multiplayer, peer to peer (?net=off; real two-device check needed)
- MULTIPLAYER section before setExposure (NET). Transport: WebRTC data channels via PeerJS 1.5.4 (cqc/vendor/peerjs, MIT; loaded only on host / join). The public PeerJS broker (0.peerjs.com, needs internet) only introduces the two browsers; the game traffic then goes directly between them (on the same WiFi, over the LAN). STUN stun.l.google.com. No TURN: two devices on very strict networks may fail to connect.
- Menu: watch / M panel cells "Multiplayer: HOST" (shows a 4-digit code; press again to leave) and "Multiplayer: JOIN a game" (prompt for the code; good on Xbox / PC), or the address ?join=1234. Host in the headset, join on the Xbox.
- Channels: 'fast' (unordered, no resends) your pose 30 Hz (slot, room-local head, yaw, pitch, eye height, gun class, dead); 'rel' shots and hits. Clock offset from the fastest packets; the other player is shown 90 ms behind (interpolated) so movement is smooth.
- The other player = a soldier (spawnEnemy, e.net: no AI, posed from his packets via enemyPose; gun smg / pistol / shotgun look by class; hidden when he holds nothing; falls when he dies, back when he respawns; respawned when he changes room or gun). Rooms match on both (fixed world seed).
- Hits: the shooter decides (enemyShot -> netHit -> his playerHit, head / body, damage x his round); his shots play on your side (flash, light, sound from his body, a trace for holes; never damage by itself). While connected no AI men spawn (game modes later).
- Checked headless with simulated packets (the broker is not reachable from the build machine): avatar spawns and follows, a hit packet takes 30 hp, a shot packet plays, no errors. Not tried between two real devices.

## CQC multiplayer modes: TOGETHER (co-op) and VERSUS (headset / Xbox check needed)
- Watch / M panel: "Multiplayer mode: TOGETHER / VERSUS" (host picks before hosting; cqc.netmode.v1), "Versus: START MATCH (host)" (also rematch, shows the score). The host's 'hi' carries the mode.
- TOGETHER: the joiner is moved into the host's safe room (frontier pulled to it, netPlaceIn when built). The host runs the AI (netHostAI); every 1/15 s it sends its men (fast channel: id, slot, room-local pos, yaw, crouch, aim, dead, loadout, look point). The joiner draws copies (e.puppet, eased, posed by enemyPose, no AI); his rounds on a copy go to the host ('eh' -> enemyShot on the real man); the host's men's shots play on the joiner ('es': flash, light, trace, sound). Each man fights the nearer player (netTargetFor, 0.8 / 1.2 margin, only within one room): inside his update NET.tgt makes headWorld / playerRayHit / playerHit / nearMiss point at the other player, and a hit is sent to him.
- VERSUS: the host picks a slot B far past anything planned (+30..430, so the map is new each match) and two kinds (room or stairwell, 35 % each); both re-plan B+1, B+2 (stair plans built by hand, levels carried on), B+3, drop caches from B, and stream only B (host's safe room), B+1, B+2, B+3 (joiner's safe room; he starts facing its entry, mirrored start spot). When both are placed the host sends go: 15 s countdown, every door locked (VS.lock in updateDoorsPre), then GO. Deaths respawn you in your own safe room, restocked; the score counts each death (vsdead). No AI.
- Known: with two safe rooms live, the single bench / workshop / locker sets follow the safe room that activated last (the other room may lack them); co-op rooms the joiner has not loaded show no copies; enemies treat the other player's body as one of theirs in some squad code.
- Checked: module parse + eslint; a two-page headless test (pages linked by a fake transport) was started.
- Xbox load: BOOT.phone is also true for an Xbox user agent, so Edge on Xbox takes the phone's light start (half-size materials, first rooms built in slices, light start) on top of Lite graphics. Reported: it would not load on an Xbox One; not reproduced here (no Xbox).
- Xbox "fatal memory exceeded": measured headless with an Xbox user agent: ~366 MB of textures (M1911 2048 px x3, guns / arms / scopes 1024 px) + ~230 MB script heap. LOWMEM (Xbox by default; ?lowmem=on|off): renderer.render / compile / compileAsync are wrapped to shrink every image texture a scene uses to at most 512 px before upload (canvas copy, the original bitmap closed; canvases, data, compressed and render-target textures left alone). Measured: 47 textures shrunk, ~153 MB of textures (366 before). Not tried on an Xbox.

## CQC title: Play on Xbox, multiplayer setup
- "Play on Xbox" (Xbox green, focused first on an Xbox): the computer game with the console profile (Lite, the light start, LOWMEM textures, pad). The profile is fixed at load: a browser that does not say "Xbox" saves cqc.device.v1 = xbox and reloads on the first press (also ?xbox); LITE.xbox / BOOT.phone / LOWMEM read it.
- Multiplayer rows: Solo / Host / Join, Mode Together / Versus (host only), the host's 4-digit code (join only); saved in cqc.mp.v1 / cqc.netmode.v1, read by netTitleBegin 1.2 s after Play (window.CQC_MP). Host: the code shows (toast + headset note) and again every 20 s while waiting; versus starts its first match on its own 2.5 s after he joins. Watch / M panel cells still work in the game.
- Checked headless with an Xbox user agent: title draws (screenshot), choices saved, Play on Xbox starts, no errors.
- Xbox loading: no all-at-once shader warm-up on the Xbox profile (XWARM false unless ?xboxwarm): the *Precompile dummies and precompile() passes are skipped, only the one hidden frame stays, so things compile when they first show (a hitch then). Xbox also turns off bounce, tfill, pdmg, hand shadow, dazzle, beam map, shelf light, trickle, muzzle blast, wisp, laser air, splash, wet steps, floor glass, shoot-out fixtures, build view, can heat, multiview, ambience, room hum, and gore mild (an OPTIONS switch set ON wins). Headless with an Xbox user agent: load 33 s -> 19 s, Play works, 117 textures / 298 geometries in use (230 / 535 before).
- Xbox detection also by Edge on Xbox's own navigator.gamepadInputEmulation (Edge on Xbox may send a desktop user agent; then none of the Xbox profile applied and the full load ran out of memory before the title was usable). "Play on Xbox" is enabled from the first moment: pressed while loading it starts the game when ready (xbPending); on a browser not seen as an Xbox it saves the profile and reloads. Root index.html passes its query on (?xbox works there). Checked headless: desktop UA + gamepadInputEmulation = Xbox profile, button usable at once, pressed during the load, the game started by itself, no errors.
- Title in two steps: 1) the device (VR / computer / Xbox / mobile), 2) Single player / Co-op / Versus, for the two multiplayer ones Hosting / Joining and the host's 4-digit code, then Start (Back returns to step 1). Saved in cqc.mp.v2; window.CQC_MP gives netTitleBegin { sel off|host|join, code, mode }. Start before the game has loaded waits and starts it when ready (not VR: the headset session needs the press). #title [hidden] forced to display none (.go's grid overrode it). Checked headless: Xbox -> Co-op -> Joining needs the code before Start, Single player starts, no errors.
- Xbox download: cqc/models/lite/ = the gun, enemy, attachment and viewmodel GLBs with textures resized to 512 px by gltf-transform 4.1.1 `resize` (formats kept: the game's own texture loader, gltfBytesPlugin). glbData / the viewmodel loader / G17A / AKMA / M1911A urls take them when LOWMEM is on (?fullmodels = the full ones). Measured with the Xbox profile: 37 MB -> 30 MB downloaded before the title is ready, Play works, no errors. The rest is mesh data (enemy 5.6 MB, M4 4.3, Glock 4.1), the 3.8 MB page, Rapier 2 MB, three 1.3 MB. The Start / Play on Xbox buttons show the loading line and percentage until ready.
- Title step 2 shows a diagnostics line (browser and version, WebGL 2 yes / NO, game script started or not, the loading line, seconds since load; the clock stops if the page freezes), for the Xbox report where nothing loaded past the menu.
- Xbox "Could not start: Cannot access 'TERM' before initialization" (user's screenshot): gfxSet runs from setTimeout 0 / DOMContentLoaded, which fire while the module waits on its first top-level await (texture generation, slow on the console); `typeof TERM` on a const still in its TDZ throws, and the start error handler aborts the load. gfxSet / yardUiSync now touch TERM and WATCH inside try, and padEmu (setInterval, reads gameStarted) returns while that is still in its TDZ. Checked headless with an Xbox user agent and the CPU throttled 4x: loads and starts in 40 s, no errors.
- Xbox load profile (CPU profile, Xbox profile): stillsShoot (title body cam stills, 3 extra scene renders + readback) 6.0 s, bloodNormalTex (canvas blur per decal kind) 2.4 s, stencilTexture 1.4 s of ~22 s CPU. On the Xbox: stills off (flag list), bloodNormalTex returns null (flat-lit blood), stencils 256 px without the per-pixel wear loop. CPU at load 22 s -> ~13 s; with the CPU throttled 6x the start went 56 s -> ~45 s, no errors. Left: three's uniform setup (2.4 s), the page / module parse before the first stage.
- Xbox "sound plays but the game never shows": the first frame after Start compiled every shader at once; on the console that blocks the page (the title stays painted, the room's sound already runs). xboxWarm (before startGame on the Xbox profile): pointer lock and sound in the press, then each visible material compiles one object at a time (compileAsync with KHR_parallel_shader_compile), "Preparing graphics n%" on the Start button, then the game starts. A throw from api.start shows on the button. Headless (Xbox UA, CPU 6x): 41 programs ready before the title goes, frames run, no errors.
- Title diagnostics line larger, with a build stamp (1109c), loading / ready, 'start queued', and the last page error (window error / unhandled rejection), to find where the Xbox start stops.
- xboxWarm can never hold the start: each compileAsync is raced with 2.5 s, the loop stops after 45 s, and the game starts after 60 s whatever happens (build 1109d).
- Xbox "Error creating WebGL context" at 0 % (user photo): the Lite GPU probe and the title's diagnostics each made a WebGL context and lost it before the game's; no probe contexts on the Xbox now (the diagnostics read WebGL2RenderingContext), and the renderer retries with lighter options (Xbox: no antialias / no high-performance). Xbox also draws straight to the screen (postEnabled false; ?xboxpost restores) and a computer frame that throws still draws (build 1109f).
- Title step 2: Single player starts the game at once (Start pressed for you); Co-op / Versus scroll Start or the code box into view (build 1109g).
- Xbox controls (report: look on the left stick, glitchy): Edge was likely still in mouse emulation (left stick = cursor = mouse look). Start sets gamepadInputEmulation = 'gamepad' inside the press; padEmu re-sets it whenever the browser reports the other mode. The 'Controller on' toast shows the pad mapping and emulation mode. View + Menu held 1 s swaps the sticks (cqc.padswap.v1) (build 1109h).
- Xbox menu and help: the watch / M menu is driven by the pad (padMenu: D-pad or left stick moves a green highlight to the nearest button that way, A presses, A on a code box asks for it, B / Menu closes); Edge stays in gamepad mode in the menu. The button-map panel hides as soon as the controller is used (hold View shows it again). Tested with a mocked pad: panel hidden, menu opens, moves, toggles, closes (build 1109i).
- Xbox freeze + white screen on picking up a gun: the pickup loaded the desktop viewmodel rig (arms GLB, gun-hand fits, gloves, sleeves) on the spot; likely out of memory on the console. The Xbox profile now adds vm / vmh / glove104 / vmsleeves = off (your procedural hands hold the gun, as ?vm=off). Headless: AKM picked up in 0.8 ms, frames keep running (build 1109j).
- Xbox black screen entering a room: auto quality there dropped levels / switched to Max FPS on the frame-rate dip of a room switching on (each change resizes the render buffers). Xbox: fixed 0.8x level, no auto changes. A lost WebGL context mid-game now shows 'The graphics ran out of memory. Reloading…' and reloads instead of staying black (build 1109k).
- Xbox menus in full screen: no help panel at start on the Xbox (hold View shows it); any pad button but View closes it; the watch menu also closes with Y or View (full-screen Edge may keep Menu) (build 1109l).
- Xbox 'Error creating WebGL context' again: Chromium blocks WebGL for a site after repeated GPU context losses (the room-entry crashes, then the automatic reload). No automatic reload on a lost context any more (a message says to quit Edge fully); a refused context now says the same; textures capped at 256 px on the Xbox; the Xbox profile is saved as soon as the console is seen (cqc.device.v1), so a later visit can't fall back to the full profile (build 1109m).
- Graphics resilience (build 1109n): the renderer is created with retries (3 option sets, 15 rounds 3 s apart, 'Waiting for the graphics' on the loader) instead of failing at once when the browser refuses a context. A lost context mid-game is recovered: preventDefault, 'Graphics reset. Recovering…' over the view, on webglcontextrestored every material / texture is marked for re-upload and the shadows redrawn, the game carries on. Headless: WEBGL_lose_context lose + restore in the safe room, the room draws again, frames continue.
- Syntax error at line 4883 on the Xbox (user report): the WebGL retry used a top-level await, which the console's Edge does not accept. Back to a plain function: lighter options first, then (if all are refused) the page reloads after 4 s, at most 10 times (sessionStorage cqc.glretry), with the reason on the loader. The module now parses as ES2020 (acorn), so older engines take it (build 1109o).

## CQC: isolated Xbox mode (cqc/xbox/xbox-mode.js; build 1109o+)
- One classic script (ES2019, no modules / top-level await) loaded before the title script and the game module; defines `window.CQCXbox` (UniversalPlatformManager) with XboxModeDetector, XboxQualityManager, XboxRendererConfig, XboxPerformanceManager, XboxDiagnostics. Every Xbox-specific path in index.html goes through `LITE.xbox`, which is now `CQCXbox.active` only (the scattered UA / gamepadInputEmulation / cqc.device.v1 checks in LITE, BOOT and the title are gone).
- Detection: UA names Xbox = confirmed; gamepadInputEmulation + Windows + no hover-capable fine pointer = probable; a Windows PC with a mouse never turns on by itself. Choice: ?xbox=on|off|auto (visit) > localStorage cqc.xbox.v1 on|off (title "Set: Auto / On / Off" button, Play on Xbox on a non-Xbox = On) > detection. The old sticky cqc.device.v1 key is deleted on load. Source shown as detected (confirmed / probable), manual, forced off.
- Profiles (?xboxprofile=, cqc.xbox.profile.v1, title Profile button, default balanced): performance / balanced / quality = render scale start / min / max, post chain on (quality only), texture cap 256 / 256 / 512, detail minPx, off-screen pose Hz, enemy LOD distances, gore, and the load-time feature switches (QS flags). Load-time parts apply on reload (they choose shader programs).
- Render scale: XboxRendererConfig snapshots the pixel ratio at attach (after initPerf), applies base x scale; applyLevel no longer touches the pixel ratio in Xbox mode; deactivate() restores the snapshot. XboxPerformanceManager: 1 s windows, bad (<54 fps or >12 % frames over 22 ms) x2 = down 0.05 / 0.1, good (>58.5, <2 % slow) x8 = up 0.05, 6 s cooldown, a step dropped from waits 30 -> 120 s before a retry, windows with a >250 ms stall ignored. Node: 40 fps -> 0.7 in 3 steps, back to 0.9 at 60 fps, an oscillating load 5 changes in 200 s, a stall ignored.
- Renderer context options in Xbox mode: high-performance, no antialias, then lighter fallbacks.
- Diagnostics overlay (off by default): Ctrl+Shift+D, ?xboxdiag, both stick clicks 1 s; mode, detection signals, profile, fps, frame ms, scale / pixel ratio, draw calls, triangles, geometries, textures, programs, GPU, WebGL2, max texture, float targets, parallel compile, heap limit.
- Checked headless: Windows PC config identical to before (programs 168, textures 234, geometries 602-603, AA + high-performance, LOWMEM off, VM on; pixel ratio differs only by the existing auto quality's timing), Xbox UA = confirmed / balanced (40 programs, 79 textures, 181 geometries, high-performance now), ?xbox=on on a PC = manual, ?xbox=off on an Xbox UA = forced off (PC config), performance (minPx 2.5, 8 Hz, 256 px) and quality (1.5, 15 Hz, 512 px, scale 1.0) profiles, live step to 0.65 and restore to the snapshot, overlay text, no page errors. Detector matrix in Node (scratch det.js): Xbox UA, Xbox with a desktop UA, PC with mouse + emulation, PC, Mac, Quest, saved on / off, address override, old key.
- Not automatic: texture atlases / KTX2 for the GLBs (offline asset work), merging more static geometry (rooms are already batched per material), real shadows (off by default for every platform: REAL_LIGHT). Quality profile turns the post chain on: unverified on the console (an earlier black screen was suspected to be the half-float post targets).
- Xbox mode uses the computer arms again (vm on; vmh / glove104 / vmsleeves stay off): xboxWarm loads all three rigs (ak, pistol2, sniper; lite GLBs, textures capped) and compiles them before the game starts ('Preparing the arms…', 25 s cap), so a pickup loads nothing. Headless Xbox UA: rigs cached at start, AKM / Glock / AWM pickups 0-3 ms with the arms shown, no errors (build 1109p).
- Multiplayer robustness (build 1109p+): the other player's body is carried into the room he enters (no rebuild per door; hidden while his room is not built here); together: both use the furthest progress for the plan (capped so your own room never becomes the safe slot) and his room, the next one and the rooms between are built on your side (within 4); out-of-order pose packets dropped; his body shows reloading (task or magazine out) and aiming vs low ready. Drops: a quiet link only ends after 60 s (a console stalls building a room or compiling), 'waiting' note after 4 s; the host keeps its code open after a drop, the joiner retries the code 6 times. Xbox warm-up also compiles the shot / grenade / flashbang effects (weaponsPrecompile in view): a first flashbang compiled them mid-game, the long freeze looked like a disconnect. Headless co-op: the joiner ran rooms 1-2, host built them, same avatar object carried.
- Flashbang still froze the Xbox: the first flash compiled the smoke / haze particle shader (the Xbox skipped polishPrecompile). xboxWarm now puts every effect group in view while it compiles (weapons, enemy, polish particles, gore, night, furniture finishes) plus a sprite with each grenade fire / flash material, then takes them back out. Headless (Xbox UA): a flashbang right after start linked no new program (was one particle program); a frag after it 0-1 (a standard material, not every run). ?dev adds flashBlast, fragBlast, NADE (build 1109q).
- Multiplayer: the VR player's real hands and gun (build 1109r). netHandsTx adds hR / hL (room-local hand targets by handedness) and gq / gh (the held gun's turn, which hand) to the pose packets; on the other side e.netH drives enemyPose: the gun sits at his real gun hand with his gun's turn, the free hand (or both, empty-handed) IK to his real hand. Headless: fake VR packets -> the avatar's wrists 0.000 m from reachable targets. Voice chat (?voice=off): the joiner calls the host over PeerJS media (echo cancellation, noise suppression), no microphone = a silent stream so you still hear him; his voice plays through the game's audio from his head (HRTF panner, muted element holds the stream). Xbox default profile is now Performance (cqc.xbox.profile.v1 / title Profile button still choose). Not tested between real devices.
- Xbox freeze after fitting a silencer: the first time a gun with a can / comp / sight was held and drawn, its parts' materials compiled mid-game (hidden parts were never warmed, and the computer arms view draws your gun under its own lights, so every gun material needs a second shader variant there). xboxWarm now compiles every gun / part / magazine / grenade prop in the room with its hidden pieces shown, once with the room's lights and once with the arms view's (VM.scene), 'Preparing guns n%'. Headless (Xbox UA): every muzzle device fitted on all five guns from the store, each gun picked up and fired: new programs 6 -> 0 (build 1109r).
- Xbox workbench: in the bench zoom (gun in the vise) the right stick moves the cursor over the parts (no look), A or RT picks what is under it (fit / take off / take the gun / the vise button), same as the mouse (build 1109s).
- Xbox cut off switching guns (M4A1-S): each gun's arms-view fit (hand bake + solve) was made at its first pickup, a long stall on the console. xboxWarm now solves every gun's fit before the start (20 s cap). Headless (Xbox UA): 20 swaps over all five guns, no leak (heap, geometries, textures flat), no new shaders at pickups (build 1109t).
- Xbox bench cursor on demand: with a gun in the vise and you at the bench, Y shows / hides a cursor (right stick moves it, A / RT fits or takes off the part under it, B hides it); a hint toast tells you; no pointer lock needed (the zoom used to need it, so on the console only the browser's own cursor worked). Y / B presses used for it don't also do their normal action (PAD.eat).
- Xbox warm-up also spawns one man of each loadout (smg, pistol, shotgun; both rifle kits), waits for their queued work, compiles them, removes them: the first man of the run built his model / gun / LODs on the spot. Headless (Xbox UA): first spawn 624 -> 15 ms (build 1109v). Parse checked; the pad cursor not run headless.
- Xbox B = menu: B tap opens the watch / M menu (hold: crouch; hop is no longer on the pad), B / Menu / Y / View close it (the closing press is eaten so its release doesn't reopen it). Xbox mode keeps a history entry in front, so Edge's Back (B in its cursor mode) never leaves the game: it re-pushes and toggles the menu instead (build 1109w). Parse checked only.
